みずほ経済 EXPRESS

対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ 
～戦略 17 分野でみた外資誘致の現状と課題～

調査部  エコノミスト 阿部大樹 
080-3709-6077 taiki.abe@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 高まる国内投資の機運。 国内供給力強化に向けては対内直接投資の取り込みも重要に 
政府は2026年7月、 日本成長戦略会議において、 国内供給力の強化に向けた官民投資ロードマップを
決定した。とりわけ経済安全保障上の重要性や成長期待が高い17の戦略分野においては、2040年度ま
でに累計370兆円超の官民投資が想定されている（図表1）。日本政策投資銀行「2026年度設備投資計
画調査」では、大企業の約58％、中堅企業の約43％が戦略分野における国内投資を強化すると答えて
おり、多くの企業が政府の方針を前向きに受け止めていることがうかがえる。 
国内投資促進の主軸を担うのは政府や国内民間企業だが、国内供給力を強化していくうえでは、外
国資本の取り込み、すなわち対内直接投資の拡大を促すことも重要だ。対内直接投資とは、外国企業
が日本企業への出資や融資を通じて、日本国内の企業経営・事業に継続的に関わることを目的とした
投資のことをいう。政府も対日直接投資推進会議で、戦略分野ごとに有望な外国企業を発掘し誘致す
る方針を掲げている。 
対内直接投資は、 投資形態の違いから四つに分類できる （図表2） 。 このうち、 「合併・買収」 や 「企

が、具体的な想定がわからない分については除外

### Page 2

2

業・財務再編」は国内供給力の強化につながらない可能性もあるが、「グリーンフィールド投資」と
「能力増強投資」は工場・生産ラインなど生産資本の増加をともない、供給網のレジリエンス向上や
国内の供給力強化につながりやすい形態だ。本稿では、これら二つの形態を合わせて「国内供給力強
化投資」と呼ぶこととする。以下では「国内供給力強化投資」に焦点を絞り、戦略分野ごとに直近5年
間の投資流入状況を確認したうえで、対内直接投資の今後の増加余地と誘致のあり方を検討する。 
■ AI・データセンター関連投資等のグローバルな機運が国内供給力強化投資をけん引  
日本の対内直接投資は、2010年代半ば以降、拡大傾向にある。国際収支統計で集計される対内直接
投資の動向を示した図表3をみると、 その力強い伸びが顕著に見て取れる。GDP比でも1.0％ 程 度（2025
年）まで増加しており、外国企業の国内投資志向が高まりつつある証左と言えよう。 
先行きは、さらなる対内直接投資の拡大が期待できそうだ。予定段階の個別投資案件を集計してい
るfDi Marketsのデータで国内供給力強化投資の動向を確認すると、2020年代に入って投資金額が大幅
に拡大していることがわかる （図表4）。同データでみられる投資拡大の動きは、クロスボーダー金融
取引の実績を計上している国際収支統計でも今後顕在化してくるとみられる。 
さらに、個別投資案件のデータを確認することで、国際収支統計では把握できなかった対内直接投
資の性質・内容を詳細に分析することが可能である。戦略分野別に国内供給力強化投資をみると、AI・
半導体分野や、データセンター関連が含まれるデジタル・サイバーセキュリティ分野で投資金額が大
きく、 かつ大幅な伸びが確認できる （図表5） 。 世界的なAIブームを受けたデータセンター関連投資の
活発化が背景とみられる。半導体製造設備やデータセンター関連の投資には巨額の資本が必要になる
ため、投資金額を件数で割った一件あたりの投資額が大規模化しやすく、大型投資案件が投資額全体
を押し上げていることがうかがえる。 
次に投資金額が大きかったのは資源・エネルギー安全保障・GX分野だ。具体的には、発電所などの
電力供給設備への投資案件が押し上げている。太陽光発電・洋上風力発電などの再生可能エネルギー
市場で海外企業の参入が増えた結果とみられ、脱炭素化を志向するグローバルな機運が追い風になっ
図表3 対内直接投資 図表4 国内供給力強化投資

3

ていると言える。加えて、電力需要の大きいデータセンターの立地を最適化するため、電力源と通信
インフラを一体的に整備する「ワットビット連携」が推進される中で、電力供給設備への投資需要が
高まっていることも当該分野の投資押上げに寄与しているとみられる。 
これらの分野では引き続きグローバルトレンドに沿った投資流入増が見込めそうである一方、造船
分野や防衛産業分野、量子分野、海洋分野の投資額は、増加しているものの依然として小規模だ。例
えば、量子分野は研究開発～実証段階の技術が中心の新領域であり、分野の将来的な成長期待は大き
い。現時点では収益化まで距離があることから、投資額の小ささ自体が問題とは言えないものの、米
国等と比べると日本の産業基盤は相対的に小さく、 スタートアップ企業の集積でも遅れを取っている。
さらに投資を呼び込むうえでは、こうした遅れを埋める手立てを講じることが必要不可欠であろう。 
また、造船分野では、2020年代に入ってから中国の存在感が一段と高まっている。日本の造船会社
による船舶の新規受注量は2000年代半ばから減少しており、世界の船舶建造量に占める日本のシェア
も低下傾向だ。背景には、中国と比べた事業所規模の小ささが指摘されており、生産コストなどの面
で水をあけられている。 
■ 幅広い分野での外国資本取り込みに向けて供給面 ・ 制度面のボトルネック軽減が重要 
戦略分野全体でみれば投資流入の堅調さが確認できた一方で、17の戦略分野それぞれにおいて投資
流入増を実現していく1ためには、 投資の門戸を開くだけでは不十分だ。 造船分野や量子分野に関して
指摘したような課題の解決に向けては、ボトルネックを軽減する地道な取り組みが必要であろう。ま
た、足元で急速に拡大しているデータセンター投資や再エネ関連投資についても、今後は成長の維持
が課題となる。 
外国資本取り込みにおける具体的なボトルネックとして、人材不足等の供給制約や複雑な制度、言
語障壁が挙げられる。日本国内の外資系企業を対象にした、日本貿易振興機構による「2025年度外資
系企業ビジネス実態調査」 では、 直近1～2年の日本のビジネス環境の課題について、 多くの企業が 「高
度人材等の確保」 と 「一般人材の確保」 を回答しているほか、 直近では 「オフィス・事業用地の確保」
図表5 戦略17分野別にみた国内供給力強化投資額の推移

ては除外。各社の個別投資プロジェクトの情報及び、日本成長戦略会議資料を基に、みずほ総合研究所調査部が分類

### Page 4

4

を回答する企業の割合も高まっている。こうした人材・立地面等の供給制約は、外資の取り込みを進
めるうえで課題となる。 
また、制度面について、日本貿易振興機構による「ジェトロ対日投資報告2018」では、外国企業が
日本でビジネス展開するうえでの阻害要因として「行政手続きの複雑さ」や「許認可制度の厳しさ」
等が挙げられ、外国企業にとって日本のビジネス環境への適応が容易ではないことがうかがえる。な
お、 同調査では 「外国語によるコミュニケーションの難しさ」 が2位にランクするなど、 言語障壁も外
国企業の参入を阻む一因だ。 
供給面・制度面のボトルネック解消に向けた対応として、人材不足については、大学等の研究・教
育機関との連携強化を通じた国内人材の育成・確保や、高度外国人材の受入強化による対応が考えら
れる。また、用地不足に対しては、産業用地の確保や空き家・空き地の有効活用を通じて、外国企業
が拠点を設立しやすい環境を整備することが重要だろう。 
制度面の対応としては、まず、手続きの一本化が挙げられる。現状、外国企業等の投資申請に際し
ては担当省庁・公的機関と個別にやり取りするケースがあり、外国企業の手続き負担は小さいとは言
えない。 この点、OECD（2026） は外国企業 ・ 投資家と関係省庁等との調整を担う 「ワンストップ窓口」
の機能強化を提言している。 言語障壁については近年通訳や英訳による手続き支援が進みつつあるが、
主要な政策等については英語アナウンスを適時提供するなど、言語障壁軽減に向けたさらなる取り組
みが求められる。

［参考文献］ 
OECD (2022), FDI Qualities Policy Toolkit, OECD Publishing 
OECD (2025), OECD Benchmark Definition of Foreign Direct Investment,  5th edition, OECD 
Publishing 
OECD (2026), OECD Economic Surveys: Japan 2026, OECD Publishing

1 コンテンツ産業のような日本企業に競争力がある分野では、外国資本の取り込みによる事業拡大も期待される一方、独自技術
や知的財産の流出リスクには留意し、慎重に議論を進める必要がある。

### Page 2

2【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
26Q4結論-上游材料加速更新，漲價仍為主旋律
1.PCB上游:供不應求推動漲價動力，新材料應用下帶來新機會
• 玻纖布:E-Glass(高階)/LowDK-2/T-Glass 在2026Q4~2027年呈現全面緊缺，成為推動當前上游材料漲價的主要
因素，也是決定結構性缺貨的關鍵原料，玻布廠迎來產品組合改善+報價上揚雙重催化。
• 銅箔:HVLP4加速驗證日台以外的供應商，中系擴產積極+中系設備加速採用下，維持2027年供需缺口放緩預期。
• 銅箔基板:PTFE樹脂為美系客戶解決互聯的確定性解方，成為銅箔基板材料中新晉路線；傳統的PPO/CH樹脂仍
有一片天，26Q4 VR300 設計將定案，PTFE+M8/M9設計呼聲較高。
• 鑽針:高階鍍膜針需求攀升，供應商在2026~2028年加快投入建置產能，新材料(PTFE)仍有一定的鑽針消耗，產
品組合的改變遠比漲價來的重要。
2. IC載板: 產能全數售罄，缺料與缺產能並行，關注供應商漲價態度。
• 供需缺口:維持2026/2027年 20%+/40%+ 缺口預期。

• 主邏輯為面積放大+層數增加，大廠產能售罄，客戶需要付出更高的價格預定產能+料源。
3. PCB:關注AWS、Google新品放量，以及2027~2028年HDI設計的變化，聚焦Tier1供應體系和伺服器
級HDI稀缺產能。
4. 建議首選關注個股:
• 高階稀缺HDI產能:2368金像電、3037欣興、4958臻鼎
• 產品組合優化顯著:1303南亞、1815富喬、8021尖點、8039台虹
• 缺貨27~28年不緩解+AI Agent受惠:8046南電、3189景碩、3037欣興、4958 臻鼎

全年核心:政策主導AI為基本方針
法案 大而美法案(One Big Beautiful Bill Act) AI行動計劃(America's AI Action Plan)
內容 1. 下調聯邦所得稅:從35%下調至21%，
減輕企業稅賦負擔。
2. 恢復2017年減稅與就業法案項目中
允許企業當年資本支出可以全額抵
減稅賦(折舊扣除)，有利重資本支出
廠商加快投資回收時間，受惠廠商
如CSP大廠。
3. 國內R&D全額費用化: 為了獎勵企業
在美國進行生產研發，符合條件的
R&D費用可以於當年度全面費用化，
受惠廠商如Open AI、Anthropic等。
1. 加速AI創新:確保美國擁有全球最強
大的AI系統，放鬆相關管制與障礙，
並鼓勵開源等。
2. 增加AI基礎設施建置:提升美國AI算
力基礎設施硬體基礎，以滿足AI推
動經濟和安全的需求；加快關鍵項
目的審核，加快建設資料中心、晶
圓代工廠、電力基礎設施，掃除一

切AI基礎設施擴充的障礙。
3. 維護國際外交與AI安全:在全球AI競
爭中掌握規則制定權，輸出美國AI
技術，同時阻止對手利用美國創新
超越美國，嚴防高階算力流向競爭
國家，向盟友出口美國AI算力技術
等，有利主權AI(Sovereign AI)的發
展。。
資料來源:福邦投顧整理
• AI 成為美國政策上的基本方針，OBBBA法案刺激企業加大投資AI算力，AAAP計畫掃除建
置AI算力的瓶頸和主權AI方針的確立。

• PCB設計日益複雜，耗用大量產能：過去伺服器板層數約16~2X層，目前主流已經來到
20~30層，未來30~50層越顯常見。
• PCB用量增加，耗用大量產能:隨著各家設計陸續參考NVIDIA Rack設計，除了原有的主
板(GPU/ASIC 使用)，亦新增更多副板，例如Switchboard、midplane等，終端客戶積極
驗證新供應廠商。

### Page 12

12【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
PCB鑽針需求呈現翻倍成長-CCL材料影響
◼ PCB用鑽針受到板材影響，在同樣板厚下，每針可鑽孔次數大幅縮水:
• M6~M7時主針鑽孔壽命約600~800次
• M8材料下可鑽400~500次
• M9材料下僅剩下100~200次
• 材料越高階，鑽針使用量會是過往的6倍以上。
過去 目前 未來
對應材質 M6~M7 M8/(有布/無布+球型矽粉) M9~M10
主針壽命 1000/600~800次 400~500次 100~200次
表5:鑽針基於不同板材的使用壽命&各應用對應的鑽針方案

### Page 13

13【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
PCB鑽針需求呈現翻倍成長-長徑比帶動報價上揚
• 當前主流GPU用PCB約5mm厚度，隨著AI用PCB板厚的提升，每增加1mm，製造上可能會採
用增加一根預鑽針，採用預鑽針預鑽至特定深度，後續用主針完成加工。
• GPU伺服器:GB200採用「1主針配2白針」→GB300採用「1主針配3白針」→VR200採用「
1主針配4白針」，
• AI用的鑽針，長度/直徑比超過30規格的鑽針，報價上會採用一個方案一個價格，因為
加工精度要求提高，難度變大；目前AI用的鍍膜鑽針報價較傳統白針多20~50%，若是
這種報價方式，特別是板厚超過7mm，產品售價將會多40~50%；未來約在2027~2028年
時，7.5~8.5mm的鑽針將成為主流，報價將會是一般白針的6倍以上報價。
• 毛利率方向:AI用的鍍膜鑽針鑽針，根據產業訪查，產品毛利率約50%+。
GPU Server ASIC Server 交換機

+1~4根預鑽白針
2主針

擠效應，白針類產品外溢給凱崴等廠商。

• 根據供應鏈調查，由於供需缺口明顯，主要CCL廠商(台光電、生益科技等)積極導入新
的供應體系，2026年主要生產廠商擴產後，可達1,200~1,300噸/月，2027年預估達
2,300~2,400噸/月。(供應體系:台銅/古河/盧森堡/金居/福田/德福/銅冠/樂天)、台系
長春後續有望驗證進入供應體系。
• 根據福邦投顧對於供需格局的假設預估，基於客戶加快認證相關可用產能，2026~2027
年的供需缺口更新預估為27%(下修)/17%(上修)。
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理預估

### Page 20

20【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
• 下個世代的半導體封裝需要更大的面積來放置更多元件、支持2D~3D封裝(CoWoS~SOIC)
，因此材料的要求需要Low CTE、高彈性變量來達到低翹曲度（Warpage)和連接可靠度
。
• 根據Resonac白皮書，為了解決內埋元件載板對於核心層的厚度精度要求，較薄的核心
層或不均勻的厚度，會導致內埋元件的損壞，因此之後許多載板核心的設計會採用厚
Core+ low CTE 膠片（採用T/Q Glass)。
• 2026年的TPU，2027年的Maia300都有見到這類核心Core CCL+PP的設計方案
(Multilayer Core)，此也會多消耗T-Glass的供應量。
T-Glass:客戶積極導入抗翹曲設計

### Page 30

30【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
無布VS.有布方向選擇
⚫ PTFE 由於Dk/Df的性能優於電子布，如果PTFE搭配電子布，會導致其Dk/Df轉差。
⚫ 未來走向:
1. 有布方案(M9~M10搭配LowDK2~4電子布):若PCB上須放置CPU/GPU/ASIC等晶片，考量尺寸
安定性因素，會需要搭配具有電子布的銅箔基板，例如UBB、ComputeBoard、
SwitchBoard、MotherBoard。
2. 無布方案(搭配無布膠片):若僅考連傳輸性能(不放置晶片)，目前採取的無布方案，樹
脂填矽粉/球型矽微粉的方案為主流，以改善膨脹性。

主要以球型粉料為主。

### Page 32

32【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
PCB製造端的製程變化-銅漿燒結
⚫ 銅漿燒結製程源於瞬態液相燒結(TLPS)技術，主要用於實現PCB盲/埋孔電路層間的Z軸
互連。
⚫ 過去傳統高多層多採用背鑽的製程，但在NV陣營，開始嘗試使用”銅漿燒結“的製程來
處理高多層版的訊號連接，以解決背鑽精度控制的問題。
⚫ 目前具有【燒結製程+大板製造能力】的主要為板廠，例如深南電路、景旺電子、勝宏
科技、滬電股份、生益電子；台系則有金像電具備此製程能力。
圖10:PCB銅燒結概念示意圖

• 目前Nvidia的供應來看，主要會以生益科技為第一供應商(合作最久)，第二供應商則從
非中國供應商中選擇，目前與生益科技與台虹入列的優先供應商，生益科技具有
CCL+PP(PTFE/CH)的製造能力，台虹PTFE CCL (PFA)可搭配生益、台光電等CCL的CH PP
方案，亦能搭配自產的PFA PP，可提供客戶彈性的產能需求。
• 其餘具有PTFE樹脂CCL開發能力的供應商，開發進度較落後，不在考量範圍內。
• PCB段的生產: 以目前客戶的意願上，採用無布方案為優先目標，PCB生產良率約在
10~30%，主要量產生產廠商預期為深南電路、景旺電子、勝宏科技、滬電股份；欣興僅
參與配合RD階段(台郡負責燒結部分,但設備有限制)。

38【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
OpenClaw:加速從LLM往AI Agent 發展
◼ OpenClaw:開放式AI助理項目，可以根據使用者指令在應用程式和線上服務之間自行處
理複雜任務，並被設計為可以代替使用者執行任務的AI軟體，並非過往的聊天機器人。
◼ OpenAI推出GPT5.4，此次更新提供Thinking和Pro兩種模型； Pro主要適用於需要進行
複雜任務的使用者，Thinking模型可以在產出答案前提出思考計畫，使用者可以從中調
整任務方向→強化Agent能力，可以支持智慧裝置執行複雜的工作流程。
◼ NVIDIA將推出類似OpenClaw的開放式平台-NemoClaw。
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理

### Page 36

39【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Agentic AI的瓶頸主要來自CPU
◼ 根據論文《A CPU-Centric Perspective on Agentic AI》一文指出，可以看出執行時間中
CPU延遲占了大比例（紅圈），最高可達到90%以上；吞吐量瓶頸除了來自GPU，亦會
來自CPU;能耗方面，大批次處理中(Batch size提升至128)，CPU平行處理效率低於GPU
，佔到總能耗超過44%，延遲會從2.8秒提升至6秒以上，超過2倍。
資料來源: 《A CPU-Centric Perspective on Agentic AI》 、福邦投顧整理
說明:Haystack RAG（問答檢索）/Toolformer（數學運算與 API 呼叫）/ChemCrow（化學文獻檢索）
Langchain（網路搜尋與摘要）/SWE-Agent（程式碼與 Bash/Python 執行)
圖11:Agent下，CPU佔據了大部分的延遲

### Page 37

40【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPU朝向多核發展→推升載板面積
◼ Agent執行:API處理、資料解析、腳本執行，90%由CPU完成，為了降低延遲，CPU往多
核心發展是趨勢。
◼ 記憶體與上下文切換:CPU需要頻繁在不同Agent狀態做切換，多核心架構能讓每個
Agent有獨立的處理單元，降低上下文切換帶來的效率損耗。
◼ 沙盒隔離/安全:Agent 需要大量獨立的一般伺服器建立Sandbox來執行，使用量與使用
者和使用量正相關，需要更多核心數、高頻寬。
資料來源:Intel、AMD、福邦投顧整理預估

傳統光通訊模組板-HDI/mSAP供應體系的新機會
◼因應高度整合和小型化需求，光模組開始大量採用MSAP來縮小產品體積和損耗；進入
1.6T，L/S 要求微縮至20µm-25µm；因此1.6T開始，強制採用MSAP。
◼材料規格上，亦往M7~M8等級以上升級，但在MSAP部分，會大量採用載體銅箔(三井金屬
90%市佔率)、日東紡的Low CTE布，目前有面臨缺料的情況。

和軟體上較強，所以選擇白牌來配合他們的結構。

◼xAI的交換機品牌主要採用Juniper的方案，主權AI體系(ex:星際之門、中東等)

交換機板:主流AI層數較低，傳統乙太網多層為主
◼ 資料中心需要承載流量大+高速傳輸，在此需求下，高多層板成為此需求的解決方案，
其優點在於電子元件間連線縮短下，訊號傳輸速度提高，而且也方遍佈線。
◼乙太網交換機為各家CSP+主權主要Scale out的主流，800G報價是400G的2倍以上，1.6T
是800G的4倍以上。

Document 056: 2026年9月社融数据点评：政府债融资支撑增强，政策成效静待释放

Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 7

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分  
1 
ooo[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 10 月 16 日      
证 券研究报告 •宏观简评报告      
数据点评  
政府 债融资支撑 增强 ，政策成效静待 释放 
——2026 年 9 月社融数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院  
[Table_Summary]  摘要：总体来看，9 月社会融资规模存量同比增长7.1%，增速较上月放缓0.1
个百分点，单月新增社融 3.55 万亿元，同比多增 177 亿元，政府债券融资大
幅多增是主要支撑。9 月政府债券融资新增 1.54万亿元， 同比多增3469亿元，
专项债发行明显提速， 前期政策性金融工具陆续投放， 财政托底作用逐步显现；
但 1—9 月政府债券净融资仍同比少增约1.16 万亿元， 全年专项债发行进度略
慢于去年， 四季度政府债融资仍有支撑。企业债券融资单月转负， 净融资减少
393 亿元，同比多减529 亿元，但 1-9 月累计净融资同比多增约1.18万亿元，
直接融资仍有一定韧性。 间接融资方面，9 月人民币贷款增加 1.27万亿元， 同
比少增 3347 亿元。企业中长期贷款同比少增幅度明显收窄，政策性金融工具
对配套融资的带动作用开始显现， 但企业投资需求仍偏弱； 居民贷款增量为0，
消费需求不足、 房地产成交走弱及提前还贷继续制约居民信贷扩张。 展望后续，
政府债融资及政策性金融工具有望继续支撑社融，企业中长期贷款或边际改
善，但居民信贷修复仍需时间，社融改善仍较依赖政策发力。存款端，9 月人
民币存款同比少增 4400 亿元，居民存款少增、非银存款同比多减，反映居民
资金配置及季末资金流动变化。9 月末 M2、M1 同比分别增长 7.2%、3.2%，
增速均有所回落，M2-M1 剪刀差扩大至 4.0 个百分点， 货币活化程度有所下降。
后续财政资金拨付和配套融资落地有望支撑企业存款， 但信贷增速下行仍制约
货币派生，预计 M2 保持一定韧性，M1 修复仍取决于实体需求改善。 
 社融存量增速放缓，增量政策效应有待释放。从社融存量看，2026年 9 月，
社会融资规模存量同比上升 7.1%，增速相比上月放缓 0.1 个百分点。从社融
增量看，9 月单月的社会融资规模增量为 3.55 万亿元，同比多增 177 亿元。9
月单月的人民币贷款增加 1.27万亿元， 同比少增 3347亿元。本月社融同比多
增主要由政府债券融资大幅多增带动， 反映出专项债发行提速、 前期政策性金
融工具陆续投放， 财政对实体经济的支持作用逐步显现。 从信贷结构看， 企业
贷款同比多增， 主要受企业中长期贷款和票据融资同比少减支撑， 低基数效应
亦有一定影响。居民贷款则继续承压，消费需求偏弱、房地产成交表现不足制
约居民加杠杆意愿，叠加提前还贷等因素，居民信贷需求仍显不足。9 月 29
日，货币与财政政策同步推出增量措施，央行下调PSL 利率并扩大支持领域，
同时增加科技创新、技术改造和支农支小再贷款额度；财政自10 月 1 日起对
符合条件的新发放首套住房商业性个人住房贷款实施贴息。两项政策有望通过
支持企业投资、 改善居民购房需求，带动企业中长期贷款和居民中长期贷款增
长，并通过财政资金投放及配套融资对社融形成支撑。四季度社融增速有望在
政府债放量与 PSL、 房贷贴息等政策工具协同发力下保持平稳， 结构上或逐步
从“政府驱动”向“政企协同”过渡。企业和居民中长期贷款有望边际改善，
但信贷需求修复仍需时间。 
 财政托底支撑政府债融资，企业债融资季节性回落。从社融结构看，2026年 9
月，直接融资新增1.56 万亿元，同比多增 3099亿元，主要受政府债券融资支
撑。从分项看，政府债融资方面，9 月政府债融资新增1.54 万亿元，同比多增
3469 亿元。今年 1-9 月，政府债券净融资约10.30万亿元， 同比仍少增约1.16

### Page 2

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
2
万亿元，显示单月融资支撑增强，但累计规模仍低于去年同期。今年财政前置
发力节奏与去年形成差异， 超长期特别国债和新增专项债在9 月迎来阶段性集
中发行，政府债券净融资对社融增速的拉动力度有所提升。截至 10 月 16 日，
今年 1.3 万亿元超长期特别国债全部发行完毕，进度与去年持平。9 月单月，
地方政府新增专项债发行 6204 亿元，同比涨幅扩大至 50.18%，发行进度
80.62%，略慢于去年同期。专项债三季度发行节奏明显提速，单季发行规模
创年内新高，但全年进度仍略慢于去年，主要原因是项目储备与资金需求之间
的匹配存在时滞。企业直接融资方面，9 月单月企业债券净融资减少 393 亿元，
同比多减 529 亿元。企业债券融资单月转负， 一方面源于9 月为信用债发行传
统淡季， 季节性因素带动发行规模回落；另一方面也由于此前企业债融资需求
集中释放后，市场进入阶段性休整。但 1-9 月企业债券净融资约 2.75万亿元，
同比多增约 1.18 万亿元，单月表现偏弱与累计融资改善并存。非金融企业境

内股票融资同比多增159 亿元至 658 亿元， 股权融资延续改善态势。表外融资
方面，表外融资增加 2479 亿元，同比少增 1100 亿元。其中，委托贷款增加
804 亿元，同比多增 521 亿元，9 月新型政策性金融工具落地为委托贷款提供
了增量支撑。信托贷款减少 478 亿元， 同比多减 540 亿元。未贴现银行承兑汇
票新增 2153 亿元，同比少增 1081 亿元。银行季末票据冲量推动票据融资从
表外转向表内，未贴现票据同比少增，主要是表内票据对表外融资替代所致。
往后看，政府债方面，截至 10月 15 日，今年地方政府专项债已发行 3.64 万
亿元，年内剩余发行额度为 7592 亿元。结合 10 月 9日公布的 5500亿元地方
政府债务限额盘活安排，四季度政府债融资仍有一定支撑。跨季后理财资金回
流、到期偿付压力减轻， 企业债净融资有望由负转正。委托贷款受益于新型政
策性金融工具“股东借款”的投放，或将延续多增。
 低基数支撑票据融资，消费与地产低迷拖累居民信贷。企业端，2026年 9 月，

企（事）业单位贷款增加 1.29 万亿元，同比多增 700 亿元。其中，企业短期
贷款增加 4600 亿元，同比少增 2500 亿元，主要源于去年短贷冲量形成的高
基数，叠加票据融资同比大幅少减，对短贷形成替代效应。企业中长期贷款增
加 8900 亿元，同比少增 200 亿元，但少增幅度已由上月的1500 亿元收窄至
200 亿元。这一改善主要源于当月政府债券供给增加、政策性金融工具对配套
贷款的撬动作用开始显现。然而在投资需求偏弱背景下， 企业扩张性融资意愿
仍显不足， 中长期信贷尚未形成有效支撑。票据融资减少 500 亿元， 同比少减
3526 亿元，主要受去年同期低基数影响（去年同期净减少4026亿元）， 同时
也体现银行季末票据冲量行为。非银贷款减少1485亿元，同比少减 863 亿元。
居民端，9 月居民贷款增量为 0， 同比多减 3890亿元。 其中， 居民短期贷款减
少 900 亿元，同比多减 2321 亿元，主要与居民消费及分期需求偏弱有关。9
月狭义乘用车零售同比降约 28%，大件消费对短贷的支撑不足；同时银行防

控风险主动收缩信用卡、消费贷供给，也对短贷形成压制。居民中长期贷款增
加 874 亿元，同比少增 1626 亿元。9 月我国 30 大中城市成交面积为652.33
万平方米，同比下降 10.75%，降幅较上月扩大 4.44个百分点，房价与收入预
期尚未趋势性改善，制约了居民加杠杆意愿。往后看，新型政策性金融工具投
放有望带动配套贷款增加， 叠加去年同期低基数， 企业中长期贷款同比或将修
复；但 10 月表内票据迎来到期高峰，可能对信贷总量形成对冲。居民端，购
房贴息政策落地带动一线成交回暖， 但低线需求偏弱， 居民中长期贷款难以大
幅回升。综合来看，10月新增信贷同比或仍少增， 结构改善优先于总量回升。
 存款结构分化加剧，货币活化程度下降。从存款端来看，2026年 9 月，人民
币存款增加 17700 亿元，同比少增 4400 亿元。分存款类型看，居民存款增加
2.4 万亿元，同比少增 5600 亿元。居民存款少增主要受两方面因素驱动：一
是债券市场阶段性走强， 部分居民存款向表外资管产品分流； 二是居民预期改

善乏力、提前还贷现象仍然存在。非金融企业存款增加 1.31 万亿元，同比多
增 3906 亿元，新型政策性金融工具加速投放，带动配套资金流入企业账户。
财政存款减少 4300 亿元，同比少减 4100 亿元。9 月政府债券净融资同比多增

### Page 3

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
3
约 3438 亿元，资金先流入财政账户导致财政存款同比少减，扣除这一因素后
仍少减约 662 亿元（4100 亿元-3438 亿元），指向财政资金拨付落地偏慢。
非银行业金融机构存款减少 1.91 万亿元， 同比多减 8500 亿元，是9 月存款端
最大拖累项。非银存款明显少增，主要因季末理财资金季节性回表，但回表规
模不及去年同期；此外，9月权益市场交投走弱、赚钱效应收敛，居民存款向
非银产品的分流节奏明显放缓。从货币端来看，9月末，M2 同比增速收窄 0.3
个百分点至 7.2%， 低于wind 一致预测水平 7.45%。本月贷款增速的持续下行
直接制约了存款货币的创造，政府债券融资规模大幅上升、 财政存款同比少减，
也在短期内压低了 M2 增速。M1 同比增速收窄 0.9 个百分点至 3.2%，低于
wind 一致预测水平 3.9%， 主要因基数扰动叠加资金活化不足。9 月末，M2-M1
同比剪刀差扩大 0.6 个百分点至 4.0 个百分点，反映出内需偏弱背景下企业投

资意愿和居民消费意愿均有待进一步提振。随着政府债券资金加快拨付、 配套
融资逐步落地，企业存款有望获得支撑，但信贷增速下行仍将制约货币派生。
M2 有望保持一定韧性，M1 回升则取决于实体需求改善，M2-M1 剪刀差短期
或维持高位。
 风险提示：政策落地节奏不及预期风险，海外经济超预期波动风险。已公布的
宏观或产业政策在实际执行过程中，可能因配套措施不完善、部门协调不畅、
地方执行力度差异或市场环境变化等因素，导致其推进速度、覆盖范围或最终
效果慢于或弱于市场预期，从而影响政策目标的实现与经济修复的进程； 主要
经济体的政治局势、经济数据或宏观政策出现意外变化，其幅度或频率超出市
场普遍预测。这种超预期波动可能引发全球金融市场动荡、 贸易条件恶化或外
需急剧收缩，并通过金融、贸易及预期渠道向国内传导，增加我国外部环境的
复杂性与不稳定性。

分析师承诺  
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规 渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明  
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准， 新三板市场以三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。  
公司评级  
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上  
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -10%与 10%之间  
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -20%与-10%之间  
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 -20%以下  
行业评级  
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%与 5%之间

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
WinWay Technology
Final point of contact: securing AI yield through
advanced testing socket
Solid growth ahead with robust capacity expansions
We initiate coverage of AI/HPC test socket leader WinWay with a Buy rating and NT
$8,900 price target. WinWay's share price has corrected 43% from its April 2026 peak
due to broader market de-rating and margin pressure associated with its Technoprobe

partnership and capacity expansion. We believe the recent correction presents an
opportunity to re-engage with WinWay's long-term growth trajectory, supported by
three key drivers: 1) increasing content from AI GPUs, CPUs, and TPUs, 2) substantial
capacity expansion, and 3) broader adoption of system-level testing (SLT). Together,
these drivers should support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E.
Importantly, WinWay's growth is increasingly being driven by higher socket content per

device rather than semiconductor unit growth alone and the stock currently trades at
16x of our 2028E P/E vs historical average of 30x, potential to re-rate with GM recovery
in the coming quarters. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Rising yields and utilisation to offset probe card dilution
Gross margin declined to 38.3% in Q226 from 45.3% in FY25, pressured by outsourced
MEMS probes and expansion-related costs. While probe-card margins are unlikely to

return to H125 levels of 48.9% given the Technoprobe partnership structure, we expect
margin pressure to ease as utilization and yields improve, supporting GM recovery to
43.1% in 2027E and 44.7% in 2028E.
CPU and TPU as fundamental growth drivers
Beyond its core GPU socket franchise,  WinWay is well positioned to benefit from the
expansion of AI-oriented CPU and TPU platforms. We expect the company to supply
sockets for AMD's Venice CPU, Nvidia's Vera CPU, and Google's TPU v8t and v9, and

also Vera CPU probe cards, providing an additional growth engine beyond GPUs.
WinWay is well positioned in this segment due to its historical collaboration with end
customers and its proximity to the local Taiwanese test supply chain. We view AI CPUs
and TPUs as an increasingly important contributor to test interface demand as compute
architectures diversify in the agentic AI era, driving WinWay's socket and probe card
sales growth of 132%/75%/69% and 94%/72%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, respectively.

We estimate CPU/TPU to contribute  around 30%/20% of WinWay's 2027E sales.
Valuation: Initiate with a Buy rating, with price target of NT$8,900
We forecast EPS of NT$101.8 in 2026E, rising to NT$201.4/393.0 in 2027E/28E. Our NT
$8,900 price target is based on 30x average 2027E-28E P/E, in line with the company's
historical average. We believe this valuation is supported by WinWay's exposure to
structural growth in AI/HPC testing, expanding socket content, and our long-term

earnings CAGR estimate of 30%. We initiate with a Buy rating.

Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will capacity constraints limit WinWay's growth in the next two to three years?
Near term, yes. Beyond 2026E, no. WinWay's current capacity remains constrained, but ongoing
expansions should significantly increase output over the next two years. With new capacity coming

online and the company shifting toward higher-value AI/HPC applications, including CPU probe
cards, >10k-pin coaxial sockets and next-generation HyperSockets, we forecast revenue growth of
105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E, supported by capacity additions, rising AI/HPC content and
expanding socket demand. We also expect operating leverage to improve as capacity scales and
utilization increases, with OPM rising from 27.5% in 2026E to 32.1%/36.7% in 2027E/28E.

Q: Will WinWay's GM recover from Q226's 38.3%?
Yes. We view recent margin pressure as largely temporary. GM has been impacted by MEMS probe
sourcing through Technoprobe and start-up costs associated with capacity expansion. Although
margin dilution from probe card business is unlikely to unravel due to the MEMS partnership terms
with Technoprobe, as new facilities ramp and yields improve, we expect profitability to recover
gradually from the Q226 trough, with gross margin improving to 43.1% in 2027E and 44.7% in

2028E.
We initiate coverage of WinWay with a Buy rating and NT$8,900 price target. We believe the market
is overly focused on near-term margin pressure and underappreciating WinWay's long-term growth
outlook. Key drivers include 1) expanding content from AI GPUs, CPUs, and TPUs, 2) substantial
capacity expansion, and 3) growing adoption of system-level testing (SLT). Together, these drivers
should support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E.

TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm  in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand as the majority are WinWay's customers. AI accelerator test times

have increased meaningfully for new platforms. We expect continued front-end and advanced
packaging investments to drive higher testing intensity and support long-term demand for advanced
sockets and test interfaces.
WinWay's share price has corrected 43% from its April 2026 peak and currently trades at 16x of our
2028E P/E vs historical average of 30x. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market
price implies only a 19% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 25%, alongside long-term GM

level of 32% vs our estimate of 45%. We see scope for re-rating.

WinWay was founded in 2001 in Kaoshiung, Taiwan. It is a semiconductor testing interface solutions
provider, with final test socket as  its main business segment. The company has high-end solutions for
AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test and burn-in test sockets and probe cards through
its own made probes and partnership with Technoprobe.

Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 1S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.
Figure 1: Semiconductor testing process: from wafer level to package level

Source: UBS
We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

content and test times rising which each iteration of new AI accelerators/CPU, more
sockets and equipment will be needed in order to increase the throughput.

WinWay is expanding capacity rapidly to capture this growing TAM. Industry test
interface suppliers are expanding capacity aggressively to meet rising AI and HPC
demand. As advanced semiconductors require higher-performance probe cards, sockets
and interface boards, capacity additions are becoming increasingly important to support

growth across both wafer-level and package-level testing.
We believe WinWay is well positioned to gain share within the high-end AI/HPC test
interface market. Taiwanese suppliers benefit from their proximity to leading fabless
customers and the growing preference for localised supply chains, particularly for
advanced testing applications where responsiveness and engineering support are
critical. Against a backdrop of tight industry supply, WinWay announced significant

capacity expansion plans over the next two to three years.
WinWay's capacity is expected by the company to increase from 3.5mn pins per month
at year-end 2025 to 6mn currently, reaching 9mn by year-end 2026 and 14mn by year-
end 2027 through expansions in Renwu, Kaohsiung. By 2027, approximately 60% of
pogo-pin requirements should be internally sourced, strengthening both supply security
and margins.
We estimate these capacity additions will support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71%

in 2026E/27E/28E.

For WinWay, this growth opportunity is substantial, as the capacity expansions for its
socket pins will satisfy around 60% of its own pogo pin  demand in 2027E, we estimate
socket sales will likely contribute for around NT$163 of EPS in 2027E and to further
expand to NT$307 in 2028E. This paves the way for substantial share gaining
opportunity for WinWay for AI/HPC sockets as we believe there are advantages to have

footprint within Taiwan, where there's already an established testing ecosystem with
testing providers such as ASE/SPIL/KYEC, and handler providers such as Hon Precision, all
positioned locally in Taiwan to provide around the clock service and support for AI/HPC
customers.
We see industry capacity tightness to support socket pin ASP growth, as we forecast pin
ASP to grow from US$2.0 in 2026E to US$2.5 in 2027E and US$3.0 in 2028E. We
believe WinWay's capacity expansion creates a significant growth opportunity with a

better margin profile. By 2027E, approximately 60% of pogo pin demand should be
internally sourced, improving both supply security and profitability. We estimate sockets
could contribute around NT$123 of EPS in 2027E, rising to NT$261 in 2028E. Combined
with its expanding capacity, strong customer relationships and proximity to Taiwan's
semiconductor testing ecosystem, including ASE/SPIL, KYEC and major handler
suppliers, WinWay is well positioned to gain share in the AI/HPC socket market.

### Page 6

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 6
The AI era has materially increased the value of semiconductor testing. Compared with
traditional mobile and automotive chips, AI accelerators incorporate larger compute
dies, more advanced nodes, chiplet architectures, advanced packaging technologies,
multiple I/O dies, and increasing HBM content. These factors drive higher-value test
consumables, including advanced MEMS probe cards, multilayer interface boards, and

next-generation sockets designed to support higher pin counts, thermal loading, and
power delivery requirements.
Capacity expansion by test interface vendors is ultimately supported by continued
investment throughout the advanced packaging ecosystem, from providers such as
TSMC, ASE, Intel, and Amkor. During the Q226 earnings calls, TSMC raised its recent
2026 capex from US$52-56bn to US$60-64bn and ASE raised its 2026 capex from US
$8.5bn to US$10.5bn, with both companies citing robust demand supporting capex

raise, we believe capex for the next two years will be even higher than 2026, indicating
continuous growth in future years. With advanced packaging technologies getting
significantly more complex and with larger form factor, illustrated by the upcoming
ramp of both TSMC's CoPoS (chip-on-panel-on-substrate) and Intel's EMIB-T
(embedded multi-die interconnect bridge) packaging technologies, test interface
solution providers will benefit with more value added consumables (see our CoPoS vs

EMIB-T deep dive).

New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC applications, are set to increase
in both front-end wafer fabrication advancements and back-end packaging
complexities with each iterations (i.e. Nvidia's roadmap from Blackwell to Rubin to

Feynman or Google's TPU roadmap from v7 to v8 to v9).   This trend is evident with
TSMC's front-end  and TSMC, Intel, and OSAT's back-end advanced packaging capacity
expansions. For front-end, we see industry N3 capacity expanding from 156kwpm in
year end 2025 to 224/283/293kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E, and N2 capacity from
47kwpm in year-end 2025 to 111/200/312kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E. For back-
end, we are seeing industry CoWoS capacity to rise from 90kwpm in 2025 to 160kwpm

in 2026E and further expand to 270kwpm in 2027E.

Final-test, system-level test, and burn-in content should continue to increase as AI

accelerators become larger, more power-hungry and more complex. Rising thermal
design power and packaging complexity increase validation requirements, driving

### Page 7

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 7
longer test times and higher demand for test equipment to prevent testing from
becoming a production bottleneck. This is where WinWay's sockets comes into play—
as high-end chips with 2.5D/3D/3.5D packaging requires intricate FT sockets to handle
the high thermal design power (TDP) and high data rates of up to 224G during the FT
process for reliable test results.

While architectural changes can affect test requirements for individual product
generations, we expect the long-term trend of rising testing content to remain intact.
For example, Nvidia's Rubin Ultra adopts a simpler package structure than previously
expected, from a 4-die structure to a 2-die structure and Rubin Ultra HBM de-spec from
HBM4E 12-Hi to initially use HBM4 8-Hi and upgrade to HBM4E 8-Hi at the latter stages,

which may moderate near-term test growth. However, future products such as Nvidia's
Feynman are expected to reintroduce greater package complexity, with a four-GPU per
package structure. More importantly, any reduction in test time can be offset by higher
production volumes, making throughput requirements equally important for testing
demand.
We expect test interface content growth to outpace unit growth, For instance, test
socket's ASP directly correlates with its pin counts, with the addition of 20-30% more

pins inside the sockets, socket ASP can grow 20-30% or more as the socket might
require significant cooling capabilities as the TDP of the DUT rises with its larger form
factor. Unlike prior semiconductor cycles, much of the current demand is driven by rising
test interface content per device rather than unit growth alone.
Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe WinWay is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers

and capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see three main growth drivers
for WinWay: 1) AI accelerators (GPU + TPU), 2) agentic AI driven CPU demand, and 3)
shift-left testing trend. Below we list the three main growth drivers in detail.
Accelerators still in focus with TPU as another significant growth driver
Nvidia has been the primary driver of testing content growth over the past several years,
benefiting the entire semiconductor testing supply chain. We expect this trend to

continue as GPU architectures evolve from Blackwell to Rubin and then to Feynman,
with increasing process node complexity, larger package sizes and more advanced
packaging technologies driving higher testing value per device.
We believe Nvidia should remain a major contributor to  WinWay, with GPUs continuing
to serve as their core growth engine. We estimate WinWay's socket share could improve
from a roughly 50/50 split with Smiths Interconnect in prior generations to 60/40 with

Rubin. Nvidia currently accounts for approximately 50% of WinWay's revenue. Both
Nvidia GPUs and Google TPUs increase testing demand through higher content per
device rather than unit growth alone.

### Page 8

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 8
Beyond Nvidia, Google's TPU roadmap represents a significant incremental growth
opportunity.  In our view, WinWay should benefit as the socket supplier for both TPU v8t
and the next-generation TPU v9 platform. We believe MediaTek's increasing share gain
for Google TPUs will also benefit the local Taiwanese test interface supply chain. We
estimate TPU to contribute to around 20% of WinWay 2027E sales.
We view TPU v9 as a particularly attractive opportunity given its large four-die package

architecture based on Intel's EMIB-T packaging technology, which should drive higher
testing content. We estimate production of 4mn TPU v8t units in 2027E, followed by
2.3mn TPU v9 packages (9.2mn dies) and an additional 2mn TPU v8t units in 2028E.
Importantly, while EMIB-T packaging is expected to be performed in Malaysia, we
believe the majority of advanced testing value should remain within the Taiwanese
testing ecosystem. Leading testing providers, including ASE and KYEC, maintain

substantial testing operations across Taiwan and Southeast Asia, supporting continued
participation by Taiwanese test interface suppliers. See our latest MediaTek report and
EMIB-T deep dive.
CPU business as a supplemental catalyst for test interface companies in
the agentic world
We believe semiconductor test interface are an underappreciated beneficiary of the
emerging agentic AI ecosystem. UBS estimates the CPU market will expand to US
$170bn by 2030E from US$31bn in 2025 and US$71bn in 2027E, driven by rising

deployment of AI infrastructure. As CPU-to-GPU ratios increasingly approach 1:1 in AI
clusters, demand for testing interfaces should expand alongside CPU volumes. TSMC is
the main CP test provider in the industry, with probe card suppliers aiding its massive
demand by supplying probes, such as TSMC's GPU probe cards supported by
Technoprobe's probes. However, with capacity now becoming scarce with strong front
and back-end demand, TSMC is outsourcing its CP business to ASE and KYEC, further

broadening the testing supply chain. This is where new probe card vendors come in—
smaller probe card players in the past, such as  WinWay, now has the opportunity to gain
significant, non-GPU share from TSMC, as they have MEMS probe capabilities
(WinWay's MEMS probe are from its strategic partnership with Technoprobe) and has a
long established relationship with the fabless companies such as Nvidia via supplying
non-probe card consumables such as sockets. Of which, CPU projects has become the

main area for  WinWay to expand their footprint.
We expect WinWay to benefit from the ramp of Nvidia's ARM-based Vera CPU and
Google's ARM-based Axion CPU in 2026E-27E. WinWay is positioned to supply sockets
and probe cards for Vera and sockets for Axion. Additional upside could come from
AMD's Venice CPU, where WinWay is well positioned. For WinWay, CPU-related
demand could account for approximately 30% of sales in 2026E-27E. Importantly, CPU
programmes diversify exposure beyond AI GPUs and provide a second structural growth

driver for test interface suppliers.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging. The
test shift-left trend will  drive structural AI/HPC probe card and test consumables

demand, with increasing test interface content as test insertions and test time increase.
WinWay should also benefit through its partnership with Technoprobe, with probe card
revenue growth remaining robust at 94%/72% in 2026E/27E following 161%/260%
growth in 2024/25.

The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while

defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed
testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million

(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall
integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of

wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge  another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level
chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

WinWay set to benefit from the AI trend with its leading
position within advanced test socket market

WinWay  was founded in 2001 in Kaohsiung, Taiwan, and is a leading provider of
semiconductor testing interface solutions, with test sockets representing its core
business. The company focuses on advanced AI/HPC applications, supplying sockets for

final test (FT), system-level test (SLT) and burn-in, as well as cobra probe cards  and
partnership with Technoprobe for MEMS probe cards.
WinWay's business is primarily divided into two segments:
 Test socket: Sockets are WinWay's largest revenue contributor. The company
supplies a broad range of testing sockets, including coaxial, PoP/plastic and burn-
in sockets, serving applications ranging from consumer electronics to high-
performance computing.

 Coaxial socket: utilised as the mainstream option for nearly all AI/HPC chips,
as traditional sockets are near its physical limits. Coaxial sockets need to handle
data rates of up to 224G for high speed transmission and high chip TDP during
the FT process.
 HyperSocket: a rubber-silicone like layer called elastomer are placed on top
of the pogo pins, these elastomer pins causes zero damage to the chips being
tested under high contact forces. WinWay's next generation HyperSocket

technology utilises elastomers to provide a softer contact between the DUT
and the pogo pins, avoiding damage to solder ball. This structure significantly
enhances spring probe's lifetime, reduces cleaning frequency, and achieves
maximum production efficiency. We estimate it will contribute around 5% of
sales in 2027E with a 20-30% ASP premium as compared to standard coaxial
socket.
 Probe cards:  WinWay historically supplies cobra probe cards (vertical probe

cards), but with the company announcing their five-year probe supply deal with
Technoprobe for MEMS probe in December 2025, they are now one of Nvida Vera
CPU's main probe card suppliers.
AI accelerators are pushing socket requirements beyond 10,000 pins, making coaxial
sockets the industry standard for leading-edge AI/HPC devices. These sockets must
support transmission speeds of up to 224G while handling significantly higher thermal
design power (TDP) during testing.

Elastomer technology is increasingly being adopted alongside spring-probe (pogo-pin)
architectures to reduce chip damage and improve contact reliability. WinWay's next-
generation HyperSocket integrates elastomer materials between the DUT and spring
probes, improving probe lifetime, reducing cleaning requirements and enhancing
production efficiency. We believe HyperSocket could become an important
differentiator as next-generation AI accelerators demand higher pin counts, bandwidth

and thermal performance. We estimate HyperSocket could contribute approximately
5% of WinWay's revenue in 2027E while carrying a 20-30% ASP premium versus
conventional coaxial sockets. The transition toward larger AI packages, higher pin
counts and faster data-transfer speeds should continue to increase socket content per
device. We expect this trend to support long-term growth in the socket market, with
technology leaders such as WinWay well positioned to gain share through next-

generation solutions including HyperSocket.
Beyond its existing product portfolio, WinWay is developing next-generation
technologies including CPO electrical test sockets and liquid-cooled sockets for future
high TDP AI accelerators, reinforcing its position within the AI/HPC testing ecosystem.

WinWay's competitive advantages are its 1) technology leadership with coaxial socket
and HyperSocket, 2) proximity to the semiconductor testing value chain in Taiwan, and
3) historical relationship with key AI companies such as Nvidia and AMD. We believe

these advantages may allow WinWay to capture significant upside in the growing
semiconductor testing TAM driven by cloud AI proliferation, and gain share across its
competition such as Smiths Interconnect for AI and HPC applications.

Compared with the probe card market, the socket industry is more fragmented.
Competition include:
 WinWay (6515.TW) : A major share gainer in AI/HPC sockets, with strong
exposure to Nvidia and AMD. The company specializes in high-performance
coaxial sockets and is developing its next-generation HyperSocket platform. It is

also expanding into probe cards through its partnership with Technoprobe.
 Leeno (058470.KQ) : Vertically integrated supplier focused on smartphone AP
and memory sockets.
 ISC (095340.KQ): Memory socket supplier with elastomer expertise.
 Smiths Interconnect (Acquired by Molex) : A key player in AI/HPC socket
industry, with its DaVinci coaxial sockets able to support high frequency
connectivity testing.
 Yamaichi (6941.T) : Japanese socket supplier with substantial automotive

exposure.
 Yokowo (6800.T): Major provider of contact probes and socket pins.
 Cohu (COHU.O) : Broad test-solutions provider spanning ATE, handlers and
sockets, with high exposure to automotive, industrial and consumer applications.

WinWay's margin is set to recover in 2027E/28E
Company GM hit a trough in Q226, at 38.3%. We view recent margin pressure as
largely temporary. GM has been impacted by MEMS probe sourcing through
Technoprobe (probe card GM now below 40% and dilutive to >50% coaxial socket GM)
and start-up costs associated with WinWay's rapid capacity expansion. During the early
part of 2025, WinWay's GM was high at 48.9% in H125, with more cobra probe cards

(60% GM) in mix with solid pogo pin and socket yield. With MEMS probe card now
accounting for more than 70% of probe card revenue and GM at around low to mid
30%, margin dilution from probe card business is unlikely to unravel for GM to return to
above 45% in the near term due to the MEMS partnership terms with Technoprobe.
However, we expect that as new facilities ramp and yields improve, profitability to
recover gradually from the Q226 trough. We see GM improving to 43.1% in 2027E and

44.7% in 2028E.

Appendix: What are semiconductor test sockets?
Semiconductor test socket are electro-mechanical interface that are used post chip
probing and chip packaging process to test the functionality of the chips. Sockets are
placed onto load boards on top of testers during the final test/system level test
processes. Handlers will place the chips onto the sockets, and pins inside the sockets will
enable the testers to read the signals from the device under test (DUT, i.e. the packaged

chips), with tester signals fan out  from beneath the PCB load board.
Figure 27: Socket illustration (with load
board underneath)
Source: UBS
As AI accelerators become larger and more complex, socket requirements continue to
increase. High-end sockets typically comprise a socket housing, alignment plate,
integrated thermal-control components and contact elements that connect the DUT to
the tester. Spring probes (pogo pins) are the most common choice for HPC sockets, with

high durability, individual probe replacement flexibility, and consistent contact force. FT
and SLT sockets account for more than 70% of the total socket market, with burn-in
sockets representing the remainder.

Source: LSEG, UBS estimates
Despite a 120% YTD share-price increase, WinWay remains 43% below its April 2026
peak and trades at only 16x our 2028E P/E. We believe the market remains overly
focused on near-term GM pressure while underestimating the company's multi-year
growth opportunity. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 19% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 25%, alongside long-term
GM level of 32% vs our estimate of 45%.

We believe investors are not fully reflecting three key drivers:
1) CPU and TPU opportunities are becoming increasingly meaningful.
Beyond its core GPU socket franchise, WinWay is positioned to benefit from Nvidia's
Vera CPU, AMD's Venice CPU, and Google's TPU. These programmes should broaden
revenue sources and create an additional demand driver for high-end sockets and probe
cards.
2) Larger AI packages are driving higher socket content per device.

As AI accelerators adopt larger form factors, higher pin counts and greater power
consumption, socket complexity and ASPs continue to rise. We expect socket content
growth to outpace semiconductor unit growth, creating a structural tailwind for
WinWay.
3) Capacity ramp and margin recovery are not fully reflected.
Following substantial capacity expansion, we expect revenue growth of
105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E. At the same time, we forecast gross margin

recovery from 38.3% in Q226 to 43.1%/44.7% in 2027E/28E as utilization and yields
improve.
Our NT$8,900 price target is based on our valuation for WinWay at 30x average 2027E-
28E P/E, in-line with its historical average of 30x and underpinned by a 30% 2027-31E
earnings CAGR. Comparing with historical NTM peer average PE of roughly 25x
(currently trading at 39x NTM PE), we believe WinWay deserves to trade at a premium
given its leadership in AI/HPC sockets, expanding CPU and TPU exposure, and our long-

term earnings CAGR estimate.
WinWay is trading at NT$6,130 as of
6 Oct 2026

38.1%/39.7% in 2027/28E, with weaker growth for both socket and probe card
businesses for slower capacity ramp up, leading to YoY sales growth of 22.3%/34.8% in

Company Description
Company Description
WinWay was founded in 2001 in Kaoshiung, Taiwan. It is a
semiconductor testing interface solution provider, with final test
sockets as its main business segment. The company has high-end
solutions for AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test
and burn-in test sockets and probe cards through its own-made
probes and a partnership with Technoprobe.

Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC
applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Company Description
WinWay was founded in 2001 in Kaoshiung, Taiwan. It is a semiconductor testing interface
solution provider, with final test sockets as its main business segment. The company has high-
end solutions for AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test and burn-in test sockets

and probe cards through its own-made probes and a partnership with Technoprobe.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value WinWay based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less

investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in WinWay
gaining/losing socket/probe card orders from major end customers, foundries, and OSATs.

- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
WinWay losing market share from major end customers, foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
WinWay, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.

- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future socket/probe card orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact WinWay’s
margins for socket/probe card shipments.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

(ESG) factors into investment process and portfolio construction. Strategies across geographies approach ESG analysis and incorporate the findings in a variety of ways.
Incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing considerations may inhibit the portfolio manager’s ability to participate in certain investment opportunities that otherwise

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

Document 149: A reprieve, not a recovery, for EU chemical producers

Source PDF: all report pdf/A reprieve, not a recovery, for EU chemical producers.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 13
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Iran war has hit EU chemicals less severely than feared,
with production, capacity utilisation and margins improving in
recent months. But renewed import competition will cloud the
outlook once Gulf oil and gas supplies to Asia recover. To reverse
the industry's long-term decline, producers and policymakers
must accelerate the green transition
Edse Dantuma
Polyethylene, the world's most common plastic, is used to make everyday
products like packaging ﬁlm, shopping bags, and bottles

Boost from softer Asian competition
The Iran war has hit European chemical producers less severely
than feared. After the ﬁrst tariﬀ shock in early 2025, demand
initially rose as customers brought forward purchases amid supply
chain uncertainty. The main boost, however, came from weaker
imports. Damage to Middle Eastern plants and Strait of Hormuz
blockades sharply reduced production and exports. About a quarter
of regional polyethylene capacity was damaged, with repairs likely

to take months.

### Page 2

Elsewhere in Asia, producers faced prolonged feedstock and energy
shortages. Asia depends far more on Gulf oil, gas and raw materials
than the EU does. Before the conﬂict, Asian countries sourced 40-
90% of their oil from the region, compared with around 20% for the
EU.
Earnings improve strongly, but production
returns to previous level
Reduced competition and tighter supply conditions drove three
consecutive months of growth in EU chemical production from

February to April, while sharp price rises provided a signiﬁcant
boost to second-quarter earnings. But the respite proved short-lived
as output contracted again as inventory restocking faded and
transport links through the Gulf improved.
By June, EU chemical production was 0.5% lower than a year
earlier and remained around 24% below early-2022 levels.
Meanwhile, exceptionally low Rhine water levels continue to disrupt
raw-material shipments and production schedules, particularly in

Germany and the Netherlands.
Modest 12-month production peak was
short-lived

### Page 3

*seasonally adjusted production, 2-month moving average
Source: Eurostat
Spain is Europe’s exception
Germany and the Netherlands have recorded the largest production
declines among the EU’s six biggest chemical producers. Together
with Belgium, they host Europe’s largest petrochemical and basic
chemicals cluster: the Antwerp-Rotterdam-Rhine-Ruhr Area. Highly
energy-intensive processes therefore weigh heavily on their
chemical sectors.
Spain stands out: chemical output has risen relatively strongly this

year and was the only top-six country to record growth in both
2024 and 2025. Its growth over the past decade, despite
ﬂuctuations, is also notable. Spain’s competitive position beneﬁts
from relatively low energy costs, a smaller share of basic chemicals,
a larger consumer chemicals segment, favourable domestic
demand and its location. A pipeline supplies Algerian gas, while EU
recovery funds have helped accelerate the expansion of renewable
power capacity.

Spain's chemical industry has held up
relatively well in recent years
Monthly production level of the chemical industry, January 2022 = 100*

### Page 4

*seasonally adjusted, 2-month moving average
Source: Eurostat
Temporary relief from competition and pressure on basic
chemicals
While the EU chemical industry has broadly been under pressure for
years, performance varies signiﬁcantly across segments. Four of
the ﬁve hardest-hit categories are in basic chemicals, which
produce base products and intermediates. Since early 2022, output
has fallen by between 25% in dyes and pigments and 47% in other
organic basic chemicals. The latter is particularly energy-intensive

and remains heavily reliant on fossil hydrocarbons for both energy
and feedstock. Compared with the previous downturn, basic
chemical producers beneﬁted from weaker international
competition in the ﬁrst half of this year. Only agricultural chemicals
fell sharply, as prolonged drought in the second quarter reduced
demand for crop protection products.
Consumer chemicals, including oils, extracts, perfumes and
toiletries, are the only segments to have grown both since early

2022 and since the start of this year. These downstream products
are less exposed to volatile energy prices and generate more value
through innovation. Speciality chemicals such as paints, coatings
and cleaning products sit between the two: output remains
structurally weak but has declined less than in basic chemicals.
Basic chemicals have been the hardest hit in
recent years

Output volume mutation per EU chemical subsector*

EU chemical activity follows the ebb and
ﬂow of the Iran war
The EU chemical production rebound was short-lived because of the
June US-Iran ceaseﬁre. Transport through the Strait of Hormuz
resumed and energy prices temporarily fell sharply. Chinese oil
reﬁning and exports of oil-based chemicals to the EU recovered. In
July, these exports were almost 40% higher than a year earlier,

while exports of organic basic chemicals – which account for about
two-thirds of China’s chemical exports to the EU – rose by nearly
50%.
Rebound of organics setting the tone for
chemical imports from China
EU imports of chemical products from China per month, % YoY

### Page 6

Source: Eurostat
Production expectations rise as Middle East conﬂict ﬂares up
again
Since the conﬂict intensiﬁed again in July, Gulf oil, gas and
feedstock shipments crucial to Asian chemical activity have fallen
sharply. Although the switch from oil to coal oﬀers a partial
alternative, this is expected to increase pressure on Asian chemical
production and exports again, which will provide some relief to
European producers.
In August and September, most EU producers therefore expected to

raise output, with expectations reaching a two-year high in
September. European chemicals will beneﬁt from weaker
competition while transport remains disrupted. However,
alternative Gulf routes will probably gain importance if the conﬂict
persists, eventually reviving Asian import competition. Oil
shipments through Hormuz have recently increased to almost 80%
of pre-war levels.
Production expectations improve again
Production expectations for the months ahead, EU chemical industry

### Page 7

Source: European Commission
Speciality and consumer chemicals have the
strongest outlook
Order books improved slightly again after a brief dip in September.
Yet producers’ assessment of orders remains much further below its
long-term average (-26% versus -14%) than production
expectations, highlighting persistent structural weakness.
The historically wide gap between upstream and downstream
activities has narrowed this year, as have sentiment diﬀerences

between countries. Sentiment improved in basic-chemicals-heavy
Germany, Belgium and the Netherlands, but weakened in France,
Italy and Spain, where basic chemicals play a smaller role. This
reﬂects basic chemicals’ temporarily improved competitive position
but also continued weak demand.
European growth remains constrained by higher inﬂation and only
a limited recovery in end markets. Higher energy prices squeeze
consumer purchasing power, hitting consumer chemicals hardest.

Speciality chemicals have better prospects as investment rises and
the AI boom lifts demand for products and materials used in:
Sustainability and electriﬁcation, including battery materials,
chemical catalysts, ﬂame retardants, lightweight resins and
composites.
Defence equipment, including reinforced plastic ﬁbres,
advanced coatings and lubricants.

### Page 8

Semiconductors, including photosensitive polymers and ultra-
pure speciality chemicals and gases.
Higher energy and feedstock costs will bite
more gradually
Chemical prices closely track energy costs, so expensive and
volatile energy will also make them higher and more volatile. Yet
the steepest rise in selling prices appears to be over. Although most
producers expected to raise prices in August and September, the
expectations index was stable just below its long-term average – a

far milder increase than after the Iran war began. This suggests
supply is gradually expanding again and price competition is
increasing.
Greater diﬃculty passing on input-cost increases will put margins
under growing pressure in the second half of the year, reinforced as
higher energy costs feed through into purchase prices. Layered
hedging – through long-term contracts, futures and other
instruments – spreads this impact over time and helped margins

improve sharply in the ﬁrst half. Large chemical companies in
particular use rolling hedging strategies, ﬁxing or mitigating prices
for diﬀerent periods: from several months to several years,
depending on the product, company circumstances and market
outlook.
Selling price expectations stayed benign in
September
Selling price expectations for the months ahead, EU chemical industry

### Page 9

Source: European Commission
Structural problems will resurface
The European chemical industry’s structural problems are far from
over. Demand has been weak for years, while competitiveness
continues to erode. European energy prices are substantially higher
than in the US and China, and government support is much lower
than in China. Once transport ﬂows normalise, global overcapacity
will again drive an inﬂux of cheap imports, especially from China.

Global chemical capacity grew by about 15% between 2018 and
2023 and, based on projects already underway, is expected to rise
by another 10% through 2028, keeping structural overcapacity in
place. Over the past decade, the EU’s share of global chemical sales
halved to 13%, while China’s nearly quintupled to 46%. The EU’s
chemicals trade deﬁcit with China widened from €21bn in 2023 to
€24bn in 2025. An artiﬁcially weak Chinese yuan and US import
tariﬀs further undermine Europe’s competitiveness.

Restructuring wave is not over
Despite somewhat better market conditions this year, restructuring
is likely to continue. Most major players have announced strategic
interventions in recent years. Earlier this year, SABIC sold its
European petrochemical operations, while Evonik is implementing a
multi-year plan of divestments, closures and reorganisations. Ineos
plans to close three UK chemical plants, and Shell wants to sell its
entire chemicals division, including a major European cluster.

European chemical capacity shrank by 30 million tonnes, or 7%,
between 2022 and 2026. Announced plant closures increased
sixfold over this period, while investment fell sharply. Petrochemical

capacity was hit hardest, down 11%, whereas specialty chemicals

Climate costs pose a growing challenge
Climate costs also put Europe at a competitive disadvantage to
countries without carbon pricing or with much lower pricing. As free

allowances are phased out, EU ETS costs will probably weigh
increasingly on production costs towards 2030, both directly
through companies’ own emissions and indirectly through higher
electricity prices. In July 2026, the European Commission proposed
extending the emissions-reduction pathway, modestly easing the
cost increase, alongside more ﬁnancial support for clean-
technology investment.
CBAM oﬀers only partial protection for EU chemicals
Fertilisers and hydrogen are covered by the CBAM levy, which

protects against imports not subject to carbon costs. In
return, free allowances for CBAM products will be phased out

### Page 11

faster, reaching zero by 2034. This will widen the cost gap
between eﬃcient, electriﬁed installations and older fossil-
based plants. Besides the faster phase-out, CBAM has several
drawbacks for chemical companies:
The levy can be circumvented when a chemical feedstock
is covered by CBAM but a product containing it is not.
This may encourage imports of ﬁnished or intermediate
products from outside the EU. The European
Commission’s proposed expansion mainly targets

downstream steel and aluminium products, not the
wider chemical value chain.
Most chemicals remain outside CBAM. Chemical value
chains involve many processes, intermediates and
feedstocks, making embedded emissions diﬃcult to
calculate. Organic chemicals have therefore been
excluded for now.
CBAM does not oﬀset the EU ETS-related competitive
disadvantage faced by EU companies in export markets.
Promising decarbonisation projects remain operationally

challenging
To regain competitiveness, European plants must decarbonise
faster through carbon capture and storage, process electriﬁcation,
and recycled and bio-based feedstocks. Although projects are
regularly cancelled, promising initiatives remain. INEOS plans to
start Project One in Antwerp this year, introducing a modern
ethylene cracker that initially emits less than half as much CO ₂  as
the EU’s most eﬃcient cracker and becomes climate-neutral within

ten years.
Spain’s Repsol aims to decarbonise its Tarragona complex through
circular chemicals and methanol, large-scale green hydrogen and
oﬀshore CO ₂  storage. In September, fertiliser producer Yara began
shipping liquid CO ₂  from Sluiskil in the Netherlands to Norway for
the permanent underground storage of 800,000 tonnes of CO ₂  a

year by Northern Lights, a partnership between Equinor, Shell and

Such projects can quickly cost hundreds of millions of euros a year.

The €5bn Project One has contributed to the doubling of INEOS’s
debt since 2021, while the group suﬀered a loss of more than
€500m in 2025. Chemicals are therefore widely seen as a hard-to-
abate sector. Governments often co-invest, but poor operating
conditions, inadequate green infrastructure and weak demand for
sustainable products still obstruct large-scale decarbonisation.
Infrastructure and decarbonisation
investment are essential
Through its European Chemicals Industry Action Plan, the European

Commission aims to prevent essential chemical production from
leaving the EU. The European Critical Chemicals Alliance brings
policymakers and producers together to identify, monitor, protect
and support critical European production. Industry supports the EU
ETS goals and system, but argues that suﬃcient free allowances
should remain until eﬀective protection against carbon leakage and
adequate demand for sustainable products are in place.
This requires supportive EU policy. Europe’s chemical

transformation is becoming a race against time: more plants will
inevitably close, leaving only the most eﬃcient and fastest-
decarbonising companies and clusters. Brussels’ growing focus on
incentives and safeguards against unwanted international
dependencies therefore oﬀers the sector welcome support.

内容提要 
2026 年 9 月份，CPI 同比增长 0.9%，较前月上涨 0.1 个百分点，这主要受能源价格上
涨推动，居民消费价格整体运行平稳，温和上涨。9 月能源价格上涨明显，是推动CPI 增速
上行的主要因素。 
2026 年 9 月份，PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较前月上涨 1.2 个百分点，上游原材料价格上涨，
尤其能源价格再次上涨， 推动工业品价格增速继续上行。 一方面，8 月以来的美伊战争加剧，
国际能源价格再次上涨； 另一方面， 促消费叠加整治反“反内卷” 式竞争， 耐用消费品价格
增速明显上涨。 
9 月国际能源价格继续上涨，中国国内通胀率上行。具体各项数据间接反映出，本月经
济结构调整继续，高技术产业继续快速增长，终端需求相对旺盛，价格增速继续上行。9 月
CPI 中通信工具价格同比增长 14.3%， 较前月上涨3.7 个百分点；PPI 中计算机、 通信和其他
电子设备制造业价格同比增长 5.8%，较前月上涨 0.5 个百分点。

能源价格波动，CPI 增速上行

涨推动，居民消费价格整体运行平稳，温和上涨。9 月能源价格上涨明显，是推动 CPI 增速
上行的主要因素。

能源价格波动，PPI 增速上行 
2026 年 9 月份，PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较前月上涨 1.2 个百分点，上游原材料价格上涨，
尤其能源价格再次上涨，推动工业品价格增速继续上行。一方面，8 月以来的美伊战争加剧，
国际能源价格再次上涨； 另一方面， 促消费叠加整治反“反内卷” 式竞争， 耐用消费品价格
增速明显上涨。 
8 月以来的美伊战争加剧，使得国际原油价格上涨，9 月国际原油平均价格明显高于 8
月，能源价格继续大幅上涨。9 月 PPI 中，石油和天然气开采业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加工
业价格分别同比增长 24.5%、21.6%，较 8 月上涨 10.0、10.5 个百分点，本月能源及相关工
业品价格增速继续明显上涨，是推动本月 PPI 增速上涨的主要因素。

北京大学国民经济研究中心
CPI 方面，深入整治 “反内卷” 式竞争或能缓解供给相对过剩的问题， 同时受 “稳增长、
促消费” 政策影响，叠加 2025 年的低基数效应， 或推动2026 年 CPI 增速上行。经济内生动
力不足， 国内经济压力依然较大， 就业、 收入预期并未发生明显改变， 居民有效需求不足问
题掣肘，抑制居民消费价格短期快速大幅上涨，但若中东战争的影响持续扩大， 或加速通胀
率的上行。综合预计 2026 年 CPI 增速或温和上涨。
PPI 方面，一方面，国内外环境进一步复杂多变，能源、矿产争夺加剧，或推动上游原
材料价格上涨，政策调整， “依法依规治理企业低价无序竞争”， 叠加低基数效应， 拉动2026
年 PPI 增速上行； 另一方面，中国国内经济压力犹存， 企业内生动力不足， 工业有效需求有
待刺激， 房地产市场产能去化犹在， 经济结构调整继续， 抑制需求端扩张。 但若中东战争的
影响持续扩大，或产生输入性通胀，工业品价格上涨或加速。综合预计 2026 年 PPI 增速或

震荡上行。

### Page 9

北京大学国民经济研究中心 
北京大学国民经济研究中心简介： 
北京大学国民经济研究中心成立于 2004 年，挂靠在北京大学经济学院。依托北京大学，重点研
究领域包括中国经济波动和经济增长、宏观调控理论与实践、经济学理论、中国经济改革实践、转轨
经济理论和实践前沿课题、政治经济学、西方经济学教学研究等。同时，本中心密切跟踪宏观经济与
政策的重大变化， 将短期波动和长期增长纳入一个综合性的理论研究框架， 以独特的观察视角去解读，
把握宏观趋势、剖析数据变化、理解政策初衷、预判政策效果。 
中心的研究取得了显著的成果， 对中国的宏观经济政策产生了较大影响。 其中最具有代表性的成
果有： （1）推动了中国人口政策的调整。中心主任苏剑教授从 2006 年开始就呼吁中国应该立即彻底
放弃计划生育政策， 并转而鼓励生育。 （2） 关于宏观调控体系的研究： 中心提出了包括市场化改革、
供给管理和需求管理政策的三维宏观调控体系。 （3） 关于宏观调控力度的研究：2017 年 7 月， 本中
心指出中国的宏观调控应该严防用力过猛， 这一建议得到了国务院主要领导的批示， 也与三个月后十
九大报告中提出的“宏观调控有度”的观点完全一致。（4）关于中国经济目标增速的成果。2013 年，
刘伟、 苏剑经过缜密分析和测算， 认为中国每年只要有6.5%的经济增速就可以确保就业。 此后不久，
这一增速就成为中国政府经济增长速度的基准目标。 最近几年中国经济的实践也证明了他们的这一测
算结果的精确性。 （5）供给侧研究。刘伟和苏剑教授是国内最早研究供给侧的学者，他们在 2007 年
就开始在 《经济研究》 等杂志上发表关于供给管理的学术论文。 （6） 新常态研究。 刘伟和苏剑合作的
论文“新常态下的中国宏观调控”（ 《经济科学》2014 年第 4 期） 是研究中国经济新常态的第一篇学术
论文。 苏剑和林卫斌还研究了发达国家的新常态。 （7） 刘伟和苏剑主编的 《寻求突破的中国经济》 被
译成英文、韩文、俄文、日文、印地文 5 种文字出版。（8）北京地铁补贴机制研究。2008 年，本课
题组受北京市财政局委托设计了北京市地铁运营的补贴机制。该机制从 2009 年 1 月 1 日开始被使
用，直到现在。 
中心出版物有： （1） 《原富》杂志。 《原富》是一个月度电子刊物，由北京大学国民经济研究中
心主办，目的是以最及时、最专业、 最全面的方式呈现本月国内外主要宏观经济大事并对重点事件进
行专业解读。 （2） 《中国经济增长报告》 （年度报告） 。该报告主要分析中国经济运行中存在的中长
期问题，从 2003 年开始已经连续出版 14 期，是相关年度报告中连续出版年限最长的一本， 被教育部
列入其年度报告资助计划。（3）系列宏观经济运行分析和预测报告。本中心定期发布关于中国宏观
经济运行的系列分析和预测报告，尤其是本中心的预测报告在预测精度上在全国处于领先地位。

Document 259: Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read

Report source: Bank of America

### Page 1

13028742     1

Economy 
Insurance costs hit the brakes 
16 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Property insurance (e.g., auto, homeowners, renters) payment growth has slowed considerably, according to Bank of America 
payments data. Median payments rose just 1% year-over-year (YoY) in 2026, easing further from the 6% growth seen in 2025, 
after a roughly 12% increase in 2024, suggesting that many consumers are getting some relief from rising insurance payments. 
• However, the moderation is uneven across consumers. Bank of America payments data suggests that Gen Z continues to 
experience insurance payment growth of nearly 6%, while increases for other generations are closer to 1% to 2%. Payments 
also rose nearly 1.5% for higher-income households but edged lower for those with lower incomes. 
• Lower accident rates, easing vehicle prices, and softer major-purchase growth may be reducing cost pressures, but geography 
remains a major dividing line. Florida metros recorded declines of roughly 7% to 8%, while California and several Northeastern 
metros continued to see relatively strong increases.

Insurance payments stabilize after years of painful growth 
The burden of higher property (e.g., auto, renters, homeowners) insurance payments may be easing for some. Notably, while the 
average payment increased 3% year-over-year (YoY), the median payment was only up 1%, according to Bank of America 
payments data. This suggests that most consumers have only seen a small increase in their premiums, though there may be a 
few outliers (Exhibit 1).  
The latest data stands in contrast to just two years ago in 2024, when most consumers saw their insurance payments surge 
about 12% YoY, after a nearly 8% YoY increase in 2023.   
Exhibit 1: Insurance payments have increased only modestly through August of this year, with growth peaking in 2024 
Median and average insurance payments (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Insurance payments by age show a similar pattern, with much stronger growth in 2024 across most generations followed by a 
cooling since then (Exhibit 2). Gen Z is the outlier here, with their payments still growing at a healthy rate, increasing almost 6% 
YoY for both 2026 and 2025, after a 7% increase in 2024. Meanwhile, Millennials and Baby Boomers experienced a 2% YoY 
increase so far this year, with Gen X only seeing around a 1% rise.

Bank of America payments data by income provides even more insight. Higher-income households paid nearly 1.5% more in 
2026 than they did a year ago, while middle-income households saw only modest increases or none at all (Exhibit 3). Conversely, 
lower-income households actually paid slightly less so far this year than they did last year.  
Exhibit 3: Higher-income households are still paying more for insurance compared to last year, while lower earners are paying less 
Median insurance payments by income cuts (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Fewer accidents and lower vehicles prices may be driving insurance payments down 
But why might insurance payments be declining at all? One thing to keep in mind is that insurance companies often raise rates 
to cover events that occurred in previous years to recover costs, while adjusting for changing risks.  
For example, the crash rate – the number of vehicle crashes per one million miles driven – suggests that there was a sharp rise 
in accidents in 2021, according to the Department of Transportation (Exhibit 4). Additionally, new and used vehicle prices 
increased significantly after the pandemic, according to Consumer Price Index (CPI) data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics 
(BLS) (Exhibit 5). So, insurers were not only facing more vehicle replacements due to a higher crash rate, but a higher cost to 
replace vehicles as well.

However, the crash rate mostly declined from 2021 to 2024, and the Department of Transportation reported that the number of 
accidents likely decreased again in 2025. Also, while the cost to maintain and repair vehicles has consistently grown since 2021, 
the price to replace vehicles peaked in 2022 and has eased since then – the latter likely a more important factor in insurance 
costs than the former. Additionally, while the CPI index for motor vehicle insurance has fallen 5% YoY, it’s possible that this may 
partially be due to rebates, according to BofA Global Research. So, the price figured into CPI may not be fully realized by the 
consumer. 
Exhibit 4: The rate of crashes is below pre- and post-pandemic

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Another factor in the divergence between a declining CPI and rising insurance payments in our data may be the growth in the 
amount of property people are insuring now. Notably though, this growth has eased in recent years. YoY growth in both new 
home and total vehicle sales peaked in mid-2023 and has grown modestly, or even declined, since (Exhibit 6). And given that 
these major purchases are still K-shaped (read more in Autos: In gear, but the “K” is still in the headlights and On the move: 
Renovation over relocation?), it could help to explain the divergence in insurance payment growth across income cohorts.  
Exhibit 6: Growth in new home and total vehicle sales peaked in mid-2023 due to a post-pandemic boom but has eased since

  I NSTI TUTE

Florida’s metros have seen the largest decline when compared to other major cities 
Examining different cities across the US also sheds light on insurance trends. Consumers in major California metros saw the 
largest increases in 2026, following near double digit growth in 2025, likely driven by widespread property damage from 
wildfires, according to BofA Global Research (Exhibit 7). Major coastal metros in the Northeast like Boston and New York also 
experienced sizable increases as well.  
Conversely, many cities throughout the Sun Belt like Houston, Nashville and Dallas saw flat or even declining insurance 
payments. BofA Global Research points to several reasons for these declines: part of the equation in this region is that the 
massive runup in the cost of property insurance and domestic migration to the region through 2024 has since eased or reversed 
course.   
Exhibit 7: California major metros have seen steady insurance payment growth, while Miami metros have seen declines 
Median insurance payments by core based statistical areas (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
In Florida, legislation that went into effect in 2023, eventually made its way into insurance prices in 2025, leading to widespread 
declines in premiums. The state also has a “lookback period” of three years, where insurance profits can be deemed too high and 
a rebate is issued to consumers. It’s possible this rebate is factoring into declines this year, as some carriers only allow rebates 
upon renewals.  
The Sunshine State was a notable standout for declines, with Orlando, Tampa and Jacksonville each down around 8% YoY in 
2026, followed closely by Miami with a 7% decrease. Additionally, Bank of America payments data suggests that Jacksonville, 
Miami and Orlando all had median insurance payments above the national level in 2023, though their payments were fell below 
that in 2026 (Exhibit 8).  
Major metros in Texas also saw easing insurance payments relative to 2023, while Seattle, San Jose, and Riverside, CA are 
among the major metros that went from below the national median to above in the three-year period ending in 2026.
